### RankMixer: Scaling Up Ranking Models in Industrial Recommenders
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15551v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Zhu, Zhifang Fan, Xiaoxie Zhu, Yuchen Jiang, Hangyu Wang, Xintian Han, Haoran Ding, Xinmin Wang, Wenlin Zhao, Zhen Gong, Huizhi Yang, Zheng Chai, Zhe Chen, Yuchao Zheng, Qiwei Chen, Feng Zhang, Xun Zhou, Peng Xu, Xiao Yang, Di Wu, Zuotao Liu
- **Abstract**: Recent progress on large language models (LLMs) has spurred interest in scaling up recommendation systems, yet two practical obstacles remain. First, training and serving cost on industrial Recommenders must respect strict latency bounds and high QPS demands. Second, most human-designed feature-crossing modules in ranking models were inherited from the CPU era and fail to exploit modern GPUs, resulting in low Model Flops Utilization (MFU) and poor scalability. We introduce RankMixer, a hardware-aware model design tailored towards a unified and scalable feature-interaction architecture. RankMixer retains the transformer's high parallelism while replacing quadratic self-attention with multi-head token mixing module for higher efficiency. Besides, RankMixer maintains both the modeling for distinct feature subspaces and cross-feature-space interactions with Per-token FFNs. We further extend it to one billion parameters with a Sparse-MoE variant for higher ROI. A dynamic routing strategy is adapted to address the inadequacy and imbalance of experts training. Experiments show RankMixer's superior scaling abilities on a trillion-scale production dataset. By replacing previously diverse handcrafted low-MFU modules with RankMixer, we boost the model MFU from 4.5% to 45%, and scale our ranking model parameters by 100x while maintaining roughly the same inference latency. We verify RankMixer's universality with online A/B tests across three core application scenarios (Recommendation, Advertisement and Search). Finally, we launch 1B Dense-Parameters RankMixer for full traffic serving without increasing the serving cost, which improves user active days by 0.2% and total in-app usage duration by 0.5%.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize the paper, provide a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, and assign a score.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RankMixer, a hardware-aware model design for scaling up ranking models in industrial recommender systems. The authors identify two primary obstacles to effectively scaling DLRMs: the need to meet strict latency and QPS constraints, and the inefficient utilization of modern GPUs by traditional CPU-era feature-crossing modules. RankMixer addresses these challenges by employing a multi-head token mixing module for feature interactions, replacing self-attention with a computationally efficient parameter-free operator, and per-token feed-forward networks (FFNs) for modeling distinct feature subspaces. A Sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) variant is also introduced for further ROI. The paper presents experimental results on a trillion-scale production dataset demonstrating RankMixer's superior scaling abilities, improving Model Flops Utilization (MFU), and allowing for a 100x increase in model parameters while maintaining similar inference latency. Online A/B tests across three core application scenarios (Recommendation, Advertisement, and Search) confirm its universality, and the deployment of a 1B Dense-Parameters RankMixer improves user active days and in-app usage duration.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a Practical Problem:** The paper directly tackles the critical issue of scaling up ranking models in industrial recommender systems, considering both performance and efficiency constraints. This is a significant and relevant problem for real-world applications.
    *   **Hardware-Aware Design:** The emphasis on hardware-aligned architectures to maximize MFU is a strong point. It demonstrates an understanding of the limitations of modern GPU hardware and aims to optimize model design accordingly. This is a departure from many academic papers that focus solely on accuracy gains without considering deployment costs.
    *   **Efficient Feature Interaction:** The multi-head token mixing module is a novel approach to feature interaction. Replacing self-attention is well-justified as standard self-attention is notoriously costly and the inner product similarity in recommendation tasks may not be as effective given the feature heterogeneity.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper presents extensive offline and online experiments on a trillion-scale production dataset, providing strong empirical evidence for RankMixer's effectiveness. The inclusion of A/B test results across different application scenarios strengthens the claim of universality.
    *   **Performance Optimization:** The optimization techniques such as Op fusion, mixed-precision inference, and custom sparse-GEMM kernels, demonstrate a commitment to practical deployability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty in Components:** While the overall architecture is well-engineered and effective, the individual components might be seen as evolutionary rather than revolutionary. Multi-head architectures, token mixing and sparse MoE are all existing ideas, albeit adapted for this specific recommender context. The novelty is in the combination and how they are specifically optimized for this workload and the specific architecture.
    *   **Limited Depth of Theoretical Analysis:** The paper focuses primarily on empirical results. A more in-depth theoretical analysis of why RankMixer scales better than other models would strengthen the contribution. Explaining the better scaling properties in relation to known scaling laws would also be useful.
    *   **Baselines Lack Recent SOTA:** Some of the baselines are not as recent as the submission date. More direct comparisons with contemporary approaches (published within the last 1-2 years) on scaling DLRMs would strengthen the empirical evaluation.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Industrial Impact:** The paper demonstrates a significant impact on a real-world recommender system (Douyin), suggesting the potential for widespread adoption. The reported gains in user engagement metrics are substantial and relevant.
    *   **Practical Guide for Scaling:** The paper provides valuable insights and practical techniques for scaling ranking models in resource-constrained industrial environments. The focus on MFU and hardware optimization is particularly valuable.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by addressing a significant practical challenge: efficiently scaling ranking models in industrial recommender systems. While the individual components may not be groundbreaking, the overall architecture, the hardware-aware design, and the comprehensive experimental validation make this a valuable contribution. The real-world deployment and the positive impact on key business metrics further solidify its significance. However, a more in-depth theoretical analysis and the inclusion of more recent baselines would further strengthen the paper.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Evaluating Text Style Transfer: A Nine-Language Benchmark for Text Detoxification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15557v1)
- **Authors**: Vitaly Protasov, Nikolay Babakov, Daryna Dementieva, Alexander Panchenko
- **Abstract**: Despite recent progress in large language models (LLMs), evaluation of text generation tasks such as text style transfer (TST) remains a significant challenge. Recent studies (Dementieva et al., 2024; Pauli et al., 2025) revealed a substantial gap between automatic metrics and human judgments. Moreover, most prior work focuses exclusively on English, leaving multilingual TST evaluation largely unexplored. In this paper, we perform the first comprehensive multilingual study on evaluation of text detoxification system across nine languages: English, Spanish, German, Chinese, Arabic, Hindi, Ukrainian, Russian, Amharic. Drawing inspiration from the machine translation, we assess the effectiveness of modern neural-based evaluation models alongside prompting-based LLM-as-a-judge approaches. Our findings provide a practical recipe for designing more reliable multilingual TST evaluation pipeline in the text detoxification case.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel automatic evaluation framework for text detoxification, a specific instance of text style transfer (TST).  It conducts a large-scale human evaluation study across nine languages (English, Spanish, German, Chinese, Arabic, Hindi, Ukrainian, Russian, and Amharic) using 20 text detoxification systems. The study compares the proposed methodology with state-of-the-art TST evaluation techniques, including LLMs-as-a-judge. The authors make their benchmark and evaluation pipeline publicly available.  They focus on fluency, content similarity, and style transfer performance (toxicity reduction), proposing improvements over existing metrics. Specifically, they address limitations of metrics like ChrF, which relies solely on surface-level n-gram overlap with reference texts and does not adequately incorporate the source toxic input. The work explores XCOMET-based metrics and new methods for content similarity and toxicity evaluation. They also extensively explore LLMs as automatic evaluators, comparing several models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Strengths:*

*   **Multilingual Scope:** The study's primary strength lies in its comprehensive multilingual focus. Most prior work in TST evaluation is limited to English, making this study a valuable contribution to the field. Evaluating across nine diverse languages provides insights into the generalizability and robustness of different evaluation metrics.
*   **Addressing Limitations of Existing Metrics:** The paper identifies and addresses the limitations of widely used metrics like ChrF, which can be overly restrictive or insufficiently sensitive to semantic preservation. The proposed improvements (e.g., in content similarity and toxicity assessment) offer promising alternatives.
*   **Extensive LLM-as-a-Judge Exploration:** The rigorous exploration of LLMs as automatic evaluators is another strong point. The paper compares different LLMs and prompting strategies, providing valuable empirical evidence on their effectiveness and limitations in the context of text detoxification.
*   **Public Availability:** Making the benchmark and evaluation pipeline publicly available is a significant boon to the research community. It facilitates reproducibility and allows other researchers to build upon this work.
*   **Comprehensive Analysis:**  The paper provides a detailed and well-structured analysis of the results, including statistical analyses and comparative evaluations across different metrics and languages.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Dataset:** While multilingual, the TEXTDETOXEVAL dataset has its limitations.  The size of the data, particularly in some languages, could affect the reliability of some conclusions. Deeper insight into the origin and diversity of the data could improve the paper.
*   **Task Specificity:**  The focus on text detoxification, while important, might limit the generalizability of the findings to other TST tasks.  The specific challenges and characteristics of detoxification could influence the performance of different metrics.
*   **Computational Resources:**  The use of large language models requires significant computational resources, which could limit the accessibility of the proposed evaluation framework for some researchers.

*Novelty and Significance:*

The paper's novelty stems from its combination of multilingual evaluation, proposed metric improvements, and comprehensive LLM-as-a-judge exploration in the context of text detoxification. The significance lies in providing a more robust and reliable evaluation framework for this important task, as well as offering insights into the performance of different metrics and LLMs across diverse languages. The publicly available benchmark promotes further research in the area.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper is likely to influence future research on TST evaluation, particularly in multilingual settings. The proposed metric improvements and LLM-based evaluation strategies could become standard practices. The public benchmark will facilitate comparisons and accelerate progress in the field.

**Score: 7.5**

*Rationale:*

The paper offers a solid contribution to the field of text style transfer evaluation, specifically within the context of detoxification.  The multilingual scope is a significant advantage, addressing a gap in existing research. The proposed improvements to evaluation metrics and the extensive LLM-as-a-judge analysis add substantial value. The limitations regarding data size, task specificity, and computational requirements are acknowledged.  These limitations do not negate the strengths of the paper, but they prevent it from achieving a higher score. It is a well-executed piece of research that offers tangible benefits to the community and has a good potential for long-term influence.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DynImg: Key Frames with Visual Prompts are Good Representation for Multi-Modal Video Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15569v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyi Bao, Chenwei Xie, Hao Tang, Tingyu Weng, Xiaofeng Wang, Yun Zheng, Xingang Wang
- **Abstract**: In recent years, the introduction of Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) into video understanding tasks has become increasingly prevalent. However, how to effectively integrate temporal information remains a critical research focus. Traditional approaches treat spatial and temporal information separately. Due to issues like motion blur, it is challenging to accurately represent the spatial information of rapidly moving objects. This can lead to temporally important regions being underemphasized during spatial feature extraction, which in turn hinders accurate spatio-temporal interaction and video understanding. To address this limitation, we propose an innovative video representation method called Dynamic-Image (DynImg). Specifically, we introduce a set of non-key frames as temporal prompts to highlight the spatial areas containing fast-moving objects. During the process of visual feature extraction, these prompts guide the model to pay additional attention to the fine-grained spatial features corresponding to these regions. Moreover, to maintain the correct sequence for DynImg, we employ a corresponding 4D video Rotary Position Embedding. This retains both the temporal and spatial adjacency of DynImg, helping MLLM understand the spatio-temporal order within this combined format. Experimental evaluations reveal that DynImg surpasses the state-of-the-art methods by approximately 2% across multiple video understanding benchmarks, proving the effectiveness of our temporal prompts in enhancing video comprehension.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "DynImg: Key Frames with Visual Prompts are Good Representation for Multi-Modal Video Understanding" introduces a novel video representation method called Dynamic-Image (DynImg). The core idea is to address the limitations of existing video understanding approaches that often lose fine-grained temporal information due to separate processing of spatial and temporal data. DynImg proposes using non-key frames as "temporal prompts" to highlight rapidly moving objects within keyframes. These prompts guide the visual encoder to pay closer attention to these dynamic regions during feature extraction. The authors also introduce a 4D video Rotary Position Embedding (ROPE) to maintain the correct spatio-temporal order within the DynImg representation. Experimental results on various video understanding benchmarks demonstrate improved accuracy compared to state-of-the-art methods, suggesting the effectiveness of their approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of using non-key frames as temporal prompts is a reasonably novel idea.  While visual prompting is used in image understanding, adapting it to the temporal domain of video and using it specifically to enhance attention to moving objects is a unique contribution. The 4D ROPE is a logical extension to handle the altered structure, but its novelty is somewhat incremental.

*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in addressing a recognized weakness of many existing video understanding models: the loss of fine-grained information of dynamic content. By focusing attention on these regions early in the processing pipeline, the paper potentially opens avenues for more accurate and efficient video understanding. The results, showing improvements across benchmarks, support the claim that this approach is worthwhile.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the issue of information loss in existing methods.
    *   **Intuitive and well-motivated approach:** The DynImg concept is intuitive and grounded in the limitations of previous methods.
    *   **Comprehensive experimental validation:** The authors evaluate their approach on a diverse set of benchmarks and perform ablation studies to understand the impact of different components.
    *   **Token efficiency:** The paper highlights the better token efficiency of DynImg compare to other methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental improvements:** While the accuracy improvements are positive, they are not groundbreaking.  A 2% increase on several benchmarks, while significant, might not revolutionize the field.
    *   **Complexity:**  The method adds complexity to the video processing pipeline, including the additional data loading for non-key frames and the 4D ROPE. The inference speed and computational cost may be a limitation. This is only touched upon briefly with loading time discussions.
    *   **Limited analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of *why* DynImg works in specific scenarios.  The qualitative analysis is also limited; showing some attention maps or other visualizations would be helpful.
    *   **Dependency on frame rate and content:** The method's effectiveness likely depends on the frame rate of the video and the nature of the motion. High frame rates might render the temporal prompts less useful, while very complex motion might still be challenging. There isn't a deep discussion about what types of video and motions benefit the most from DynImg.

*   **Potential Influence:** DynImg could influence future research in video understanding by highlighting the importance of early spatio-temporal interaction and inspiring new methods for incorporating temporal information into visual feature extraction. It might also spur further research on visual prompting in the video domain.

*   **Missing information:**

    *   A comparison of inference time would be valuable in addition to the loading time discussion.
    *   Information on the number of parameters for the modified modules would be useful for assessing its resource use.

**Overall Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of **7**.

**Rationale:**

While the paper addresses an important problem and proposes a novel solution, the improvements are incremental rather than transformative. The complexity of the approach and the limited analysis of its benefits in specific scenarios slightly temper the overall evaluation. The results are positive and well-validated, demonstrating that the DynImg representation is a valuable contribution, but more analysis and deeper insights are needed to fully understand its potential and limitations. The token efficiency and performance boost do help the paper achieve a solid rating.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning to Extract Rational Evidence via Reinforcement Learning for Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15586v1)
- **Authors**: Xinping Zhao, Shouzheng Huang, Yan Zhong, Xinshuo Hu, Baotian Hu, Min Zhang
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) effectively improves the accuracy of Large Language Models (LLMs). However, retrieval noises significantly impact the quality of LLMs' generation, necessitating the development of denoising mechanisms. Previous methods extract evidence straightforwardly without explicit thinking, which risks filtering out key clues and struggles with generalization. To this end, we propose LEAR, which learns to extract rational evidence by (1) explicitly reasoning to identify potential cues within retrieval contents first, and then (2) consciously extracting to avoid omitting any key cues helpful for answering questions. Specifically, we frame evidence reasoning and evidence extraction into one unified response for end-to-end training; apply knowledge token masks for disentanglement to derive reasoning-based and extraction-based answers; and devise three types of verifiable reward functions, including answer, length, and format, to update the model via the policy optimization algorithm. Extensive experiments on three benchmark datasets show the effectiveness of LEAR, providing compact and high-quality evidence, improving the accuracy of downstream tasks, and promoting effective application in online RAG systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, along with a score and justification:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the issue of noise in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems and proposes a novel reinforcement learning (RL) based approach called LEAR (LEArns to extract Rational evidence) for extracting rational evidence. LEAR aims to improve the accuracy of LLMs by (1) explicitly reasoning to identify potential cues within retrieved content and (2) consciously extracting evidence to avoid omitting key information useful for answering questions.  It frames reasoning and extraction as a unified response, employs knowledge token masks for disentanglement, and uses verifiable reward functions (answer, length, format) to guide policy optimization. Experiments on three benchmark datasets (NQ, TQA, HotpotQA) demonstrate the effectiveness of LEAR in providing compact, high-quality evidence, improving downstream task accuracy, and promoting effective application in online RAG systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a novel combination of ideas rather than ground-breaking individual concepts. Framing evidence extraction as an RL problem is not entirely new (other self-aligned methods are also present), but the explicit incorporation of reasoning *before* extraction is a valuable contribution. The specific design of the reward functions and the knowledge token masking technique also add incremental novelty. The idea of disentangling reasoning and extraction with knowledge tokens to guide the rewards during the RL training is novel.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant and well-recognized problem in RAG, which is the presence of noise in retrieved content. The demonstrated improvements in accuracy, efficiency, and robustness (against noise) are valuable contributions to the field. The comparative analysis with existing evidence extraction methods highlights the benefits of the proposed approach. The gains observed are reasonably good on the experimented datasets.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined approach with a good balance of algorithmic components (reasoning, extraction, RL, rewards, disentanglement).
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple datasets.
    *   Thorough ablation studies and robustness analysis.
    *   Improved retrieval performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary. It is mostly a clever combination of existing ideas.
    *   The reward functions, while effective, are relatively simple and hand-crafted.
    *   The results, while promising, do not represent a massive leap in performance over all existing methods. The gains over "Full" might be dataset dependent.
    *   The gains in inference efficiency are not fully fleshed out beyond a slightly faster inference efficiency than baseline methods.
    *   The RL setup is not directly compared with imitation learning or a supervised learning approach.
    *   There is no direct comparison in zero-shot or few-shot settings compared to other prompting/fine-tuning methods in RAG.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging more research into explicitly reasoning-based evidence extraction methods for RAG. The knowledge token masking and reward function design could be adopted and further refined by other researchers.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid and well-executed piece of research that addresses a relevant problem in retrieval-augmented generation. While the individual components are not entirely novel, the combination of reasoning-based extraction, RL training, knowledge token masking, and verifiable reward functions is a worthwhile contribution. The comprehensive experimental evaluation supports the claims made in the paper. However, the incremental nature of the novelty prevents it from receiving a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SegDT: A Diffusion Transformer-Based Segmentation Model for Medical Imaging
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15595v1)
- **Authors**: Salah Eddine Bekhouche, Gaby Maroun, Fadi Dornaika, Abdenour Hadid
- **Abstract**: Medical image segmentation is crucial for many healthcare tasks, including disease diagnosis and treatment planning. One key area is the segmentation of skin lesions, which is vital for diagnosing skin cancer and monitoring patients. In this context, this paper introduces SegDT, a new segmentation model based on diffusion transformer (DiT). SegDT is designed to work on low-cost hardware and incorporates Rectified Flow, which improves the generation quality at reduced inference steps and maintains the flexibility of standard diffusion models. Our method is evaluated on three benchmarking datasets and compared against several existing works, achieving state-of-the-art results while maintaining fast inference speeds. This makes the proposed model appealing for real-world medical applications. This work advances the performance and capabilities of deep learning models in medical image analysis, enabling faster, more accurate diagnostic tools for healthcare professionals. The code is made publicly available at \href{https://github.com/Bekhouche/SegDT}{GitHub}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SegDT: A Diffusion Transformer-Based Segmentation Model for Medical Imaging":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SegDT, a new segmentation model designed for medical imaging, specifically skin lesion segmentation.  SegDT leverages a Diffusion Transformer (DiT) architecture optimized for low-cost hardware. The model incorporates Rectified Flow to improve generation quality and reduce inference steps compared to standard diffusion models. The authors evaluate SegDT on ISIC datasets (2016, 2017, 2018) and compare its performance against existing segmentation methods. The experiments show state-of-the-art performance and fast inference times. The authors highlight SegDT's efficiency in terms of GFLOPs and parameters, emphasizing its suitability for resource-constrained environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the *specific combination* of existing technologies rather than groundbreaking innovation in any single component. Leveraging a diffusion transformer (DiT) is becoming increasingly common in medical imaging. The utilization of rectified flow to accelerate inference is an existing technique applied to a specific task.  The most significant contribution is optimizing a DiT specifically for low-cost hardware while maintaining accuracy.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a *practical problem*: enabling high-performance medical image segmentation on readily available, less expensive hardware. The focus on reducing inference time is also very important for clinical usability. Demonstrating competitive performance with fewer computational resources and faster inference makes the model potentially more accessible and impactful. The state-of-the-art performances of the method over existing methods make it more significant.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents thorough experimental results, showcasing SegDT's performance on standard datasets and comparing it to multiple established methods.
    *   **Focus on Efficiency:** The emphasis on GFLOPs, parameters, and inference time is valuable, acknowledging the limitations of deploying computationally intensive models in real-world clinical settings.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly states the problem being addressed and the motivations behind the proposed solution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Advancement:** While the *combination* of DiT and rectified flow is effective, it's not a completely new architectural concept.
    *   **Limited Qualitative Analysis:** While segmentation masks are displayed, a deeper dive into failure modes (e.g., specific types of lesions where SegDT struggles) would strengthen the analysis. The qualitative analysis in the paper mentioned limitations with irregular borders or small lesions but this could be studied further.
    *   **Hardware Specificity:** The paper mentions optimization for "low-cost" GPUs. The experiments are performed on NVIDIA RTX 3090 GPUs, however, which are relatively high-end.  More discussion about the definition of "low cost" and comparison to models optimized for truly low-power devices (e.g., mobile GPUs) would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   **Accessibility:** By reducing the computational cost, SegDT has the potential to make advanced medical image segmentation tools available to a wider range of healthcare providers.
    *   **Real-time Applications:** The faster inference time opens the door for real-time or near-real-time diagnostic applications.

**Justification:**

SegDT represents a solid engineering effort that combines existing techniques to create a more efficient and practical medical image segmentation model. It advances the field by making state-of-the-art performance accessible on a broader range of hardware, which is a significant concern for real-world deployment. The approach isn't revolutionary in terms of novel architectures, but is good in terms of overall performance and efficiency.
